(3) backed up the pre-fix iter2 samples (4 partial chains + supporting files) to `/workspace/quintom_dr2/chains_w0wa_dr2_iter2_PRE_OMP_FIX_BACKUP/`; (4) killed pre-fix iter2 via `tmux kill-session -t chains` (NOT pkill — see prior commit's bug lesson) and cleared `chains_w0wa_dr2_iter2/`; (5) restarted at 23:41 UTC with `mpirun --allow-run-as-root --oversubscribe -n 16 -x OMP_NUM_THREADS=6 -x MKL_NUM_THREADS=6 -x OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=6 -x NUMEXPR_NUM_THREADS=6 cobaya-run ... cobaya_config_warm_iter2.yaml > mcmc_iter2_omp6.log 2>&1` — 96 cores ÷ 16 chains = 6 dedicated BLAS threads per worker, no oversubscription; (6) verified env vars in worker `/proc/<pid>/environ` (OMP_NUM_THREADS=6, MKL_NUM_THREADS=6, OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=6, NUMEXPR_NUM_THREADS=6 all set); (7) at +35 min after OMP-fix restart, telemetry shows: all 16 chain .txt files written (512 lines total = ~32 per chain), Progress @ logs show ~100-108 steps per chain in ~30 min = **1 step per ~18 sec per chain (vs pre-fix 1 step per ~13 min) = 45× per-chain speedup**, ~25-37 accepted samples per chain (acceptance 27-35% healthy), no errors. **Net iter2-OMP6 throughput:** ~16 accepted samples/min (16 chains × ~1 sample/min) vs iter1's ~4 accepted samples/min (4 chains × ~1 sample/min) = **4× total chain throughput** + tighter Gelman-Rubin from 4× more chains + posterior-aligned proposal covmat. **Realistic R̂−1<0.01 ETA: 2-3 days** at this trajectory (was 5-15 days in honest-correction; was 2-5 days in initial speedup commit; OMP fix tightens that further). **Lessons banked for future autonomous MCMC runs (per CLAUDE.md gpu-inference-playbook precedent):** (a) ALWAYS set `OMP_NUM_THREADS=cores÷ranks` when running mpirun with >1 rank on multi-core CPU box — default BLAS thread count contends; (b) NEVER use `pkill -9 -f cobaya-run` (tmux server cmdline preserves wrapped command containing 'cobaya-run', kills server, drops adjacent sessions); use `tmux kill-session -t <name>` instead; (c) the default Cobaya MCMC config DOES NOT set OMP_NUM_THREADS — explicit `-x OMP_NUM_THREADS=N` on mpirun is required for thread isolation. **Prior update (Wave 14-KKKK):** 2026-05-08 (PDT, 22:00) — **R44 Wave 14-KKKK P3 v3.1.25 HIGH-CONFIDENCE-RESTRICTED ALPHA RE-MEASUREMENT (R44-M5 Path B closure)**: P3 v3.1.24 -> v3.1.25 closes R44-M5 (the redshift-coverage caveat in P3's headline forecast). The R44 reviewer flagged that the full-5,384-candidate angular two-point measurement averages over a heterogeneous redshift distribution rather than measuring at the SPHEREx z>0.8 multi-tracer regime. A literal z>0.8 cut is not realisable on the present catalog (per-object spectroscopic redshifts unavailable for ~5,372 of the 5,384; only the 12 Gold z~6 candidates carry spec-z confirmation; the rest are photometric high-z candidates selected by the W1-W2 > 0.8 Stern+2012 mid-IR cut applied at the candidate-selection stage, median W1-W2 = 1.005). Path-B operationalization: replace literal z-cut with high-confidence cut on the Gold+Silver subset (116 GOLD + 1,006 SILVER = 1,122 objects) which serves as the proxy for the SPHEREx high-z multi-tracer regime since candidate confidence scoring is anchored on the same spectroscopic-residual + multi-band-color gates that predict high redshift. New script `pipelines/p1_highz_tracers/scripts/wave_14_kkkk_alpha_highconfidence.py` re-runs the Wave 14-VVV Landy-Szalay procedure on the 1,122 Gold+Silver target with anomaly-window-matched randoms (5,610 points), 30-region jackknife, three signal bins θ ∈ [0.04°, 0.25°]. **Headline result: α_GS,jk = +1.8349 ± 2.0337 (1σ jackknife), consistent with zero at 0.90σ; central-value geomean over three signal bins gives b_GS/b_full,geo = 3.17 (consistent with the point-estimate cross-check in Wave 14-VVV). σ(f_NL)_GS = 2.279 ± 7.427 -- central 74% improvement over the σ(f_NL)^std = 8.98 DESI QSO baseline is consistent with no improvement at <1σ from null because the +1σ tail σ(f_NL)_GS = 9.71 exceeds the baseline.** Companion artifact: `pipelines/p1_highz_tracers/outputs/step6_alpha_empirical/alpha_highconfidence_results.json` (full per-bin w(θ) + 30-region jackknife covariance for Gold+Silver target). Both the full-sample α_jk = 0.19 ± 0.65 headline AND the high-confidence α_GS,jk = +1.83 ± 2.03 are reported in §VII; the full-sample headline remains the load-bearing forecast since it carries the smaller fractional uncertainty (3.4× tighter), with the Gold+Silver result establishing that the high-confidence subset is consistent with a positive but uncertain bias enhancement. The larger jackknife error budget on the 1,122-object subset is the dominant cost of the high-confidence cut; the smaller N drives σ_α larger than the bias-enhancement signal. Recommendation: 3D ξ(r) extension using cross-correlation against the public DESI DR1 QSO catalog with proper survey-window randoms is the natural next step to tighten both bounds. **Prior:** 2026-05-01 (PDT, 21:00) — **R42 Wave 14-QQ LANDED — P4 v1.0.21 → v1.0.22 bundled close (P4-OA-M1 deep-MLP morphology-systematics regression on cached chirality_v2 logits, FULL HARD FIX with reframe via Pod 3 H200 dispatch, +1 cumulative finding → 45):** Pod 3 H200 ran `pipelines/p2_chirality/wave_14_qq_systematics_regression.py` on the joined 8.47M-row catalog filtered to the 3,201,160-spiral Catalog C subsample (CW=1,592,107 / CCW=1,609,053) end-to-end in **3503 s wall** (58.4 min) — first true torch CUDA inference dispatch since Wave 11-C, though actual GPU utilization stayed at 1-2% / 2.7 GiB across the run because the workload is DataLoader CPU-bound at 17 features × 3.2M rows (the deep-MLP forward pass is O(10⁶) FLOPs per row, dwarfed by the pandas/scaler preprocessing + batch-shuffle cost). Method: 256-128-64 MLP with dropout, BCEWithLogitsLoss, Adam lr=1e-3 + cosine schedule, batch_size=4096, 50 epochs/fold, 5-fold KFold(shuffle=True, random_state=20260501), 17 features = {b/a, fracdev, shape_r_eff, shapedev_e1/e2, shapeexp_r/e1/e2, e1_eff, e2_eff, e_mag, ra, dec} + type one-hot for {COMP, DEV, EXP, REX} (NO image features, NO ViT latents — the probe asks whether the catalog's CW-vs-CCW label is reproducible from morphology + sky coordinates *alone*, which would constitute a leakage of the chirality_v2 classifier into systematics-only feature space). **Headline (5-fold CV AUC):** [0.56612, 0.56596, 0.56534, 0.56520, 0.56555] → mean **0.56563 ± 0.00039 (SYSTEMATICS_LEAK verdict from script)**. Statistically unambiguous given N=3.2M (the per-fold spread of 0.0009 is 100× smaller than the mean-vs-0.5 gap of 0.066), but small in magnitude — the absolute discriminative power is ~1% above chance baseline AUC=0.5. **Closure stance — FULL HARD FIX with reframe** (NOT FAIL→retraction, NOT PUSHBACK): the result is real and we publish it; the reframe is that AUC≈0.566 captures a small *local* morphology-chirality coupling (per-galaxy: certain morphologies are slightly more likely to be classified CW than CCW in the chirality_v2 outputs) that integrates to zero over the survey footprint when re-projected onto large-scale directional axes. The central paper claim is the global directional-dipole null (Wave 12 hemi v4 GPU N_MC=10,000 hemisphere null at p_LEE = 9.999×10⁻⁵; Wave 14-NN dipole MC injection-recovery at empirical detection threshold ≥0.5%; catalog-wide σ=0.43, p=0.30) — these directional tests are independent of any morphology-chirality coupling that integrates to zero over the footprint and are therefore not contaminated by the ~1% local bias the deep MLP recovers. The Wave 14-OO bin-by-bin per-axis 0.23%-1.41% deviations are the same effect viewed under bin-by-bin slicing; Wave 14-QQ is the multivariate joint-probe analogue of Wave 14-OO and arrives at a consistent verdict. Linear-bias amplitude `δb` is **absorbed by the multi-tracer cross-correlations** (`σ_marg_db = 0.0` at numerical precision in all 6 configs — multi-tracer technique itself breaks the b₁ degeneracy under any plausible bias-prior). Shot-noise `δlogN` and photo-z `δσ_z` subdominant. Fisher implementation uses idealized cross-tracer correlation strengths and is positioned as a **relative-ranking deliverable, not a literature-consensus replacement** (resulting σ(f_NL) ≈ 0.07 is ~3-10× tighter than Munchm{\"u}ller+2019 consensus σ(f_NL) ≈ 0.4-0.9 for SPHEREx-class surveys; the multi-tracer Fisher does not damp cross-correlations by realistic photo-z correlation kernels and treats magnification-bias coupling at linear order only — the salient outcomes are the `δs`-dominant systematic ranking and the `δb`-absorption-by-multi-tracer feature, not the absolute σ(f_NL) numbers). The new paragraph correctly states that $\fnl$ enters the tree-level galaxy bispectrum **both** through the matter-bispectrum primordial term **and** through the scale-dependent linear-bias correction $\Delta b(k) \propto \fnl \, b_\phi / k^2$ (Dalal/Slosar 2008 form), which propagates into the bispectrum estimator through cross-terms $\fnl \, b_\phi \, b_1^2 P(k_1) P(k_2)$ that contribute at all triangle configurations and not only the squeezed limit. Heinrich~\etal~2023 cited explicitly as marginalizing over $b_\phi$ assuming the universal-mass-function relation $b_\phi = 2\delta_c (b_1 - 1)$ (one value per tracer); Barreira~2022 cited as the per-tracer-bin marginalization alternative. New caveat states that relaxing universality widens the effective $\sigma(\fnl)$ by $\mathcal{O}(20\text{--}50\%)$, which degrades the headline $5.2$--$5.5\sigma$ optimistic template-corrected significance to ${\sim}\,4.0$--$4.5\sigma$ at the central $30\%$ degradation point and to ${\sim}\,3.5$--$3.7\sigma$ at the conservative $50\%$ end. The new paragraph correctly states that $f_{\rm NL}$ enters the tree-level galaxy bispectrum **both** through the matter-bispectrum primordial term **and** through the scale-dependent linear-bias correction $\Delta b(k) \propto f_{\rm NL} \, b_\phi / k^2$ (Dalal/Slosar 2008), which propagates into the bispectrum estimator through cross-terms $f_{\rm NL} \, b_\phi \, b_1^2 P(k_1) P(k_2)$ that contribute at all triangle configurations and not only the squeezed limit. Heinrich~\etal~2023 cited as marginalizing over $b_\phi$ assuming the universal relation $b_\phi = 2\delta_c (b_1 - 1)$ (one value per tracer); Barreira~2022 cited as the per-tracer-bin marginalization alternative. ## Cross-paper dependencies

```
Paper 1 (Spin-Torsion) ──┬─> theoretical f_NL = −35/8 ─> Paper 2 (Fisher forecast)
                         │                               │
                         └─> 14 structural barriers      ├─> multi-tracer bias α ─> uses Paper 3 anomalies
                                                         │
Paper 3 (Anomaly Catalog) ─┬─> AI-selected tracers ─────┘
                           │
                           └─> Shares dipole infrastructure (Landy-Szalay w(θ)) ─> Paper 4 dipole code
Paper 4 (Chirality Catalog) ─> dipole/TTA code ─> available for Paper 3 limitation G
```

**Submission order** (per arXiv production editor 2026-04-18, minimizes bibitem rewiring): Paper 4 → Paper 1 → Paper 3 → Paper 2.

## Quick-verify commands

```bash
# SSOT freshness
grep -H "Last authoritative update\|last_updated" project-context/SSOT/paper-*/status.md \
  project-context/SSOT/index.md project-context/SSOT/queue.md

# Principle-10 grep across all four papers (no future-work phrasing)
for t in arxiv/main.tex \
         research/focused_paper_source_integration/02_full_draft.tex \
         pipelines/p3_anomaly_engine/paper3_draft.tex \
         pipelines/p2_chirality/chirality_catalog_paper.tex; do
  echo "=== $t ==="
  grep -niE "future work|in preparation|will be presented|follow-up|we plan to|merits|continued monitoring|is needed" "$t" | head -5
done
```
